class Table(Queryable):
    """
    Tables should usually be initialized using the ``db.table(table_name)`` or
    ``db[table_name]`` methods.

    The following optional parameters can be passed to ``db.table(table_name, ...)``:

    :param db: Provided by ``db.table(table_name)``
    :param name: Provided by ``db.table(table_name)``
    :param pk: Name of the primary key column, or tuple of columns
    :param foreign_keys: List of foreign key definitions
    :param column_order: List of column names in the order they should be in the table
    :param not_null: List of columns that cannot be null
    :param defaults: Dictionary of column names and default values
    :param batch_size: Integer number of rows to insert at a time
    :param hash_id: If True, use a hash of the row values as the primary key
    :param hash_id_columns: List of columns to use for the hash_id
    :param alter: If True, automatically alter the table if it doesn't match the schema
    :param ignore: If True, ignore rows that already exist when inserting
    :param replace: If True, replace rows that already exist when inserting
    :param extracts: Dictionary or list of column names to extract into a separate table on inserts
    :param conversions: Dictionary of column names and conversion functions
    :param columns: Dictionary of column names to column types
    :param strict: If True, apply STRICT mode to table
    """

    #: The ``rowid`` of the last inserted, updated or selected row.
    last_rowid: Optional[int] = None
    #: The primary key of the last inserted, updated or selected row.
    last_pk: Optional[Any] = None

    def __init__(
        self,
        db: Database,
        name: str,
        pk: Optional[Any] = None,
        foreign_keys: Optional[ForeignKeysType] = None,
        column_order: Optional[List[str]] = None,
        not_null: Optional[Iterable[str]] = None,
        defaults: Optional[Dict[str, Any]] = None,
        batch_size: int = 100,
        hash_id: Optional[str] = None,
        hash_id_columns: Optional[Iterable[str]] = None,
        alter: bool = False,
        ignore: bool = False,
        replace: bool = False,
        extracts: Optional[Union[Dict[str, str], List[str]]] = None,
        conversions: Optional[dict] = None,
        columns: Optional[Dict[str, Any]] = None,
        strict: bool = False,
    ):
        super().__init__(db, name)
        self._defaults = dict(
            pk=pk,
            foreign_keys=foreign_keys,
            column_order=column_order,
            not_null=not_null,
            defaults=defaults,
            batch_size=batch_size,
            hash_id=hash_id,
            hash_id_columns=hash_id_columns,
            alter=alter,
            ignore=ignore,
            replace=replace,
            extracts=extracts,
            conversions=conversions or {},
            columns=columns,
            strict=strict,
        )

    def __repr__(self) -> str:
        return "<Table {}{}>".format(
            self.name,
            (
                " (does not exist yet)"
                if not self.exists()
                else " ({})".format(", ".join(c.name for c in self.columns))
            ),
        )

    @property
    def count(self) -> int:
        "Count of the rows in this table - optionally from the table count cache, if configured."
        if self.db.use_counts_table:
            counts = self.db.cached_counts([self.name])

END STEP

returncode=0
    def reset_counts(self) -> None:
        "Re-calculate cached counts for tables."
        tables = [table for table in self.tables if table.has_counts_triggers]
        with self._conn_context():
            self._ensure_counts_table()
            counts_table = self.table(self._counts_table_name)
            counts_table.delete_where()
            counts_table.insert_all(
                {"table": table.name, "count": table.execute_count()}
                for table in tables
            )

    def execute_returning_dicts(
        self, sql: str, params: Optional[Union[Sequence, Dict[str, Any]]] = None
    ) -> List[dict]:
        return list(self.query(sql, params))

src = open(path).read()
old = '''    def execute_returning_dicts(
        self, sql: str, params: Optional[Union[Sequence, Dict[str, Any]]] = None
    ) -> List[dict]:
        return list(self.query(sql, params))
'''
new = '''    def enable_safe_import(self) -> None:
        """
        Enable safe import mode for this database. While enabled,
        :meth:`create_import_checkpoint` can be used to create rollback
        checkpoints.
        """
        self._safe_import_enabled = True

    def disable_safe_import(self) -> None:
        """
        Disable safe import mode. Existing checkpoints are unaffected, but no
        new checkpoints can be created until safe import mode is enabled again.
        """
        self._safe_import_enabled = False

    def create_import_checkpoint(self) -> str:
        """
        Create a new rollback checkpoint and return its opaque id.

        Raises :class:`SafeImportNotEnabledError` if safe import mode has not
        been enabled. Nested checkpoints are supported.
        """
        if not self._safe_import_enabled:
            raise SafeImportNotEnabledError(
                "Safe import mode is not enabled - call enable_safe_import() first"
            )
        checkpoint_id = uuid.uuid4().hex
        savepoint_name = "cp_{}".format(checkpoint_id)
        self.execute('SAVEPOINT "{}"'.format(savepoint_name))
        self._active_checkpoints[checkpoint_id] = savepoint_name
        self._checkpoint_order.append(checkpoint_id)
        return checkpoint_id

    def _checkpoint_not_active_or_missing(self, checkpoint_id: str) -> None:
        if checkpoint_id in self._finalized_checkpoints:
            raise CheckpointNotActiveError(
                "Import checkpoint {} has already been committed or rolled back".format(
                    checkpoint_id
                )
            )
        if checkpoint_id not in self._active_checkpoints:
            raise CheckpointNotFoundError(
                "Import checkpoint {} does not exist".format(checkpoint_id)
            )

    def rollback_to_checkpoint(self, checkpoint_id: str) -> None:
        """
        Roll back the database - including schema changes - to the state it was
        in when ``checkpoint_id`` was created.
        """
        self._checkpoint_not_active_or_missing(checkpoint_id)
        idx = self._checkpoint_order.index(checkpoint_id)
        savepoint_name = self._active_checkpoints[checkpoint_id]
        self.execute(
            'ROLLBACK TO SAVEPOINT "{}"'.format(savepoint_name)
        )
        self.execute('RELEASE SAVEPOINT "{}"'.format(savepoint_name))
        # Any checkpoints created after this one are destroyed too
        for later_id in self._checkpoint_order[idx:]:
            self._active_checkpoints.pop(later_id, None)
            if later_id not in self._finalized_checkpoints:
                self._finalized_checkpoints.add(later_id)
        self._checkpoint_order = self._checkpoint_order[:idx]

    def commit_checkpoint(self, checkpoint_id: str) -> None:
        """
        Commit a checkpoint, making all of its changes permanent and finalizing
        the checkpoint id.
        """
        self._checkpoint_not_active_or_missing(checkpoint_id)
        idx = self._checkpoint_order.index(checkpoint_id)
        savepoint_name = self._active_checkpoints[checkpoint_id]
        self.execute('RELEASE SAVEPOINT "{}"'.format(savepoint_name))
        # Nested checkpoints are released along with this one
        for later_id in self._checkpoint_order[idx:]:
            self._active_checkpoints.pop(later_id, None)
            if later_id not in self._finalized_checkpoints:
                self._finalized_checkpoints.add(later_id)
        self._checkpoint_order = self._checkpoint_order[:idx]

    def cleanup_checkpoint(self, checkpoint_id: str) -> None:
        """
        Remove all tracking for a checkpoint. If the checkpoint is still active
        it is released (committed) first so no dangling savepoint remains.
        """
        if checkpoint_id in self._active_checkpoints:
            savepoint_name = self._active_checkpoints[checkpoint_id]
            try:
                self.execute('RELEASE SAVEPOINT "{}"'.format(savepoint_name))
            except OperationalError:
                pass
            idx = self._checkpoint_order.index(checkpoint_id)
            self._checkpoint_order = [
                cid for cid in self._checkpoint_order if cid != checkpoint_id
            ]
            self._active_checkpoints.pop(checkpoint_id, None)
            return
        if checkpoint_id in self._finalized_checkpoints:
            self._finalized_checkpoints.remove(checkpoint_id)
            return
        raise CheckpointNotFoundError(
            "Import checkpoint {} does not exist".format(checkpoint_id)
        )

    def _ensure_invariants_table(self) -> None:
        with self._conn_context():
            self.execute(_IMPORT_INVARIANTS_CREATE_SQL.format(_IMPORT_INVARIANTS_TABLE))

    def add_import_invariant(self, table: str, sql: str) -> str:
        """
        Add a persistent import invariant for ``table`` and return an opaque
        invariant id.
        """
        self._ensure_invariants_table()
        invariant_id = uuid.uuid4().hex
        with self._conn_context():
            self.execute(
                "INSERT INTO {} (\"table\", \"id\", \"expression\") VALUES (?, ?, ?)".format(
                    quote_identifier(_IMPORT_INVARIANTS_TABLE)
                ),
                [table, invariant_id, sql],
            )
        return invariant_id

    def remove_import_invariant(self, table: str, invariant_id: str) -> None:
        """
        Remove an import invariant for ``table``. Missing invariants are
        ignored.
        """
        if not self._invariants_table_exists():
            return
        with self._conn_context():
            self.execute(
                "DELETE FROM {} WHERE \"table\" = ? AND \"id\" = ?".format(
                    quote_identifier(_IMPORT_INVARIANTS_TABLE)
                ),
                [table, invariant_id],
            )

    def _invariants_table_exists(self) -> bool:
        return self.table_names(fts4=False, fts5=False).__contains__(
            _IMPORT_INVARIANTS_TABLE
        ) or bool(
            self.execute(
                "SELECT 1 FROM sqlite_master WHERE type = 'table' AND name = ?",
                [_IMPORT_INVARIANTS_TABLE],
            ).fetchone()
        )

    def list_import_invariants(self, table: str) -> List[Dict[str, str]]:
        """
        Return a list of ``{"id": ..., "expression": ...}`` dictionaries for
        the import invariants attached to ``table``.
        """
        if not self._invariants_table_exists():
            return []
        rows = self.execute(
            "SELECT \"id\", \"expression\" FROM {} WHERE \"table\" = ? ORDER BY \"id\"".format(
                quote_identifier(_IMPORT_INVARIANTS_TABLE)
            ),
            [table],
        ).fetchall()
        return [{"id": row[0], "expression": row[1]} for row in rows]

    def _is_aggregate_expression(self, expression: str) -> bool:
        return bool(_AGGREGATE_RE.search(expression))

    def _evaluate_invariant(self, table: str, expression: str) -> bool:
        stripped = expression.strip()
        if stripped.upper().startswith("SELECT"):
            row = self.execute(stripped).fetchone()
            if row is None or len(row) == 0:
                return False
            return bool(row[0])
        if self._is_aggregate_expression(expression):
            row = self.execute(
                "SELECT {} FROM {}".format(expression, quote_identifier(table))
            ).fetchone()
            if row is None or len(row) == 0:
                return False
            return bool(row[0])
        # Non-aggregate expressions must be true for every row
        rows = self.execute(
            "SELECT {} FROM {}".format(expression, quote_identifier(table))
        ).fetchall()
        return all(bool(row[0]) for row in rows)

    def validate_import_invariants(
        self, table: str
    ) -> Dict[str, Any]:
        """
        Validate all import invariants for ``table``.

        Returns ``{"valid": bool, "failures": [...]}`` where each failure is a
        ``{"id": ..., "expression": ..., "error": ...}`` dictionary.
        """
        failures = []
        for invariant in self.list_import_invariants(table):
            try:
                ok = self._evaluate_invariant(table, invariant["expression"])
                if not ok:
                    failures.append(
                        {
                            "id": invariant["id"],
                            "expression": invariant["expression"],
                            "error": "Invariant expression returned a false value",
                        }
                    )
            except Exception as e:
                failures.append(
                    {
                        "id": invariant["id"],
                        "expression": invariant["expression"],
                        "error": str(e),
                    }
                )
        return {"valid": len(failures) == 0, "failures": failures}

    def _tables_with_invariants(self) -> List[str]:
        if not self._invariants_table_exists():
            return []
        rows = self.execute(
            "SELECT DISTINCT \"table\" FROM {}".format(
                quote_identifier(_IMPORT_INVARIANTS_TABLE)
            )
        ).fetchall()
        return [row[0] for row in rows]

    def _format_invariant_failure_report(self, failures: List[Dict[str, str]]) -> str:
        lines = [
            "{} import invariant(s) failed validation:".format(len(failures))
        ]
        for failure in failures:
            lines.append(
                "  - {}: {} ({})".format(
                    failure["id"], failure["expression"], failure["error"]
                )
            )
        return "\\n".join(lines)

    def run_safe_import(
        self,
        table: Optional[str],
        strict: bool,
        fn: Callable[[], None],
        tables: Optional[List[str]] = None,
    ) -> Dict[str, Any]:
        """
        Run ``fn`` inside a rollback checkpoint, validate import invariants,
        and commit only on success.

        Returns ``{"success": True}`` or, for non-strict mode,
        ``{"success": False, "checkpoint_id": ..., "failures": [...],
        "error_report": ...}``. In strict mode failures roll back and raise.
        """
        self.enable_safe_import()
        checkpoint_id = self.create_import_checkpoint()
        old_safe_active = self._safe_import_active
        self._safe_import_active = True
        try:
            try:
                fn()
            except Exception as e:
                self._safe_import_active = old_safe_active
                try:
                    self.rollback_to_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)
                    self.cleanup_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)
                except Exception:
                    pass
                if strict:
                    raise
                return {
                    "success": False,
                    "checkpoint_id": checkpoint_id,
                    "failures": [],
                    "error_report": str(e),
                }
            self._safe_import_active = old_safe_active
            tables_to_validate = tables if tables is not None else (
                [table] if table is not None else self._tables_with_invariants()
            )
            failures: List[Dict[str, str]] = []
            for t in tables_to_validate:
                failures.extend(self.validate_import_invariants(t)["failures"])
            if not failures:
                self.commit_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)
                self.cleanup_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)
                return {"success": True}
            self.rollback_to_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)
            self.cleanup_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)
            error_report = self._format_invariant_failure_report(failures)
            if strict:
                raise ImportInvariantError(error_report)
            return {
                "success": False,
                "checkpoint_id": checkpoint_id,
                "failures": failures,
                "error_report": error_report,
            }
        finally:
            self._safe_import_active = old_safe_active

    def execute_returning_dicts(
        self, sql: str, params: Optional[Union[Sequence, Dict[str, Any]]] = None
    ) -> List[dict]:
        return list(self.query(sql, params))
'''

OBSERVATION:
returncode=0
        Apply SQLite string quoting to a value, including wrapping it in single quotes.

        :param value: String to quote
        """
        # Normally we would use .execute(sql, [params]) for escaping, but
        # occasionally that isn't available - most notable when we need
        # to include a "... DEFAULT 'value'" in a column definition.
        return self.execute(
            # Use SQLite itself to correctly escape this string:
            "SELECT quote(:value)",
            {"value": value},
        ).fetchone()[0]

    def quote_fts(self, query: str) -> str:
        """
        Escape special characters in a SQLite full-text search query.

        This works by surrounding each token within the query with double
        quotes, in order to avoid words like ``NOT`` and ``OR`` having
        special meaning as defined by the FTS query syntax here:

        https://www.sqlite.org/fts5.html#full_text_query_syntax

        If the query has unbalanced ``"`` characters, adds one at end.

        :param query: String to escape
        """
        if query.count('"') % 2:
            query += '"'
        bits = _quote_fts_re.split(query)
        bits = [b for b in bits if b and b != '""']
        return " ".join(
            '"{}"'.format(bit) if not bit.startswith('"') else bit for bit in bits
        )

    def quote_default_value(self, value: str) -> str:
        if any(
            [
                str(value).startswith("'") and str(value).endswith("'"),
                str(value).startswith('"') and str(value).endswith('"'),
            ]
        ):
            return value

END STEP

    def iterdump(self) -> Generator[str, None, None]:
        "A sequence of strings representing a SQL dump of the database"
        if iterdump:
            yield from iterdump(self.conn)
        else:
            try:
                yield from self.conn.iterdump()
            except AttributeError:
                raise AttributeError(
                    "conn.iterdump() not found - try pip install sqlite-dump"
                )

        table: str,

        safe_mode: bool = False,
        strict: bool = False,
        **kwargs: Any,
    ) -> Any:
        """
        Import records from a CSV file (a path string, ``pathlib.Path`` or text
        file-like object) into ``table``.

        With ``safe_mode=True`` the import is wrapped in a rollback checkpoint,
        import invariants are validated afterwards and the operation commits
        only on success.
        """
        if isinstance(source, (str, pathlib.Path)):
            f = open(source, newline="", encoding="utf-8-sig")
            should_close = True
        else:

        try:

                    records, strict=strict, **kwargs
                )

import sqlite_utils
from sqlite_utils.db import Database
db = Database(memory=True)
# Add an invariant on a table that doesn't exist yet - always false for a SELECT? use aggregate on non-existent table => error failure
# Let's instead create table first with invariant COUNT(*) <= 1, then safe insert creates triggers/indexes via separate operations inside fn
db['t'].create({'id': int}, pk='id')
inv = db.add_import_invariant('t', 'COUNT(*) <= 1')
def op():
    db['t'].create_index(['id'], unique=True)
    db.execute('CREATE TRIGGER trg AFTER INSERT ON t BEGIN SELECT 1; END')
    db['t'].insert_all([{'id': 2}, {'id': 3}])
result = db.run_safe_import('t', False, op)
print('result success:', result['success'])
print('rows:', db['t'].count)
print('indexes:', [i.name for i in db['t'].indexes])
print('triggers:', [t.name for t in db.triggers])

returncode=0
def insert(
    path,
    table,
    file,
    pk,
    flatten,
    nl,
    csv,
    tsv,
    empty_null,
    lines,
    text,
    convert,
    imports,
    delimiter,
    quotechar,
    sniff,
    no_headers,
    encoding,
    batch_size,
    stop_after,
    alter,
    detect_types,
    no_detect_types,
    analyze,
    load_extension,
    silent,
    ignore,
    replace,
    truncate,
    not_null,
    default,
    strict,
):
    """
    Insert records from FILE into a table, creating the table if it
    does not already exist.

    Example:

        echo '{"name": "Lila"}' | sqlite-utils insert data.db chickens -

    By default the input is expected to be a JSON object or array of objects.

    \b
    - Use --nl for newline-delimited JSON objects
    - Use --csv or --tsv for comma-separated or tab-separated input
    - Use --lines to write each incoming line to a column called "line"
    - Use --text to write the entire input to a column called "text"

    You can also use --convert to pass a fragment of Python code that will
    be used to convert each input.

    Your Python code will be passed a "row" variable representing the
    imported row, and can return a modified row.

    This example uses just the name, latitude and longitude columns from
    a CSV file, converting name to upper case and latitude and longitude
    to floating point numbers:

    \b
        sqlite-utils insert plants.db plants plants.csv --csv --convert '
          return {
            "name": row["name"].upper(),
            "latitude": float(row["latitude"]),
            "longitude": float(row["longitude"]),
          }'

    If you are using --lines your code will be passed a "line" variable,
    and for --text a "text" variable.

    When using --text your function can return an iterator of rows to
    insert. This example inserts one record per word in the input:

    \b
        echo 'A bunch of words' | sqlite-utils insert words.db words - \\
          --text --convert '({"word": w} for w in text.split())'
    """
    try:
        insert_upsert_implementation(
            path,
            table,
            file,
            pk,
            flatten,
            nl,
            csv,
            tsv,
            empty_null,
            lines,
            text,
            convert,
            imports,
            delimiter,
            quotechar,
            sniff,
            no_headers,
            encoding,
            batch_size,
            stop_after,
            alter=alter,
            upsert=False,
            ignore=ignore,
            replace=replace,
            truncate=truncate,
            detect_types=detect_types,
            no_detect_types=no_detect_types,
            analyze=analyze,
            load_extension=load_extension,
            silent=silent,
            not_null=not_null,
            default=default,
            strict=strict,
        )
    except UnicodeDecodeError as ex:
        raise click.ClickException(UNICODE_ERROR.format(ex))


@cli.command()
@insert_upsert_options(require_pk=True)
def upsert(
    path,
    table,
    file,
    pk,
    flatten,
    nl,
    csv,

END STEP

    delimiter,
    quotechar,
    sniff,
    no_headers,
    encoding,
    batch_size,
    stop_after,
    alter,
    upsert,
    ignore=False,
    replace=False,
    truncate=False,
    not_null=None,
    default=None,
    detect_types=None,
    no_detect_types=False,
    analyze=False,
    load_extension=None,
    silent=False,
    bulk_sql=None,
    functions=None,
    strict=False,
):
    db = sqlite_utils.Database(path)
    _register_db_for_cleanup(db)
    _load_extensions(db, load_extension)
    _maybe_register_functions(db, functions)
    if (delimiter or quotechar or sniff or no_headers) and not tsv:
        csv = True
    if (nl + csv + tsv) >= 2:
        raise click.ClickException("Use just one of --nl, --csv or --tsv")
    if (csv or tsv) and flatten:
        raise click.ClickException("--flatten cannot be used with --csv or --tsv")
    if empty_null and not (csv or tsv):
        raise click.ClickException("--empty-null can only be used with --csv or --tsv")

                docs = (fn(doc) or doc for doc in docs)

        extra_kwargs = {

            "replace": replace,
            "truncate": truncate,

            "strict": strict,

            extra_kwargs["not_null"] = set(not_null)

            extra_kwargs["defaults"] = dict(default)

            extra_kwargs["upsert"] = upsert

        docs = (decode_base64_values(doc) for doc in docs)

                doc_chunks = chunks(docs, batch_size)
            else:
                doc_chunks = [docs]

                with db.conn:
                    db.conn.cursor().executemany(bulk_sql, doc_chunk)
            return

                docs, pk=pk, batch_size=batch_size, alter=alter, **extra_kwargs
            )
        except Exception as e:
            if (
                isinstance(e, OperationalError)
                and e.args
                and (
                    "has no column named" in e.args[0] or "no such column" in e.args[0]
                )
            ):
                raise click.ClickException(
                    "{}\n\nTry using --alter to add additional columns".format(
                        e.args[0]
                    )
                )
            # If we can find sql= and parameters= arguments, show those
            variables = _find_variables(e.__traceback__, ["sql", "parameters"])
            if "sql" in variables and "parameters" in variables:
                raise click.ClickException(
                    "{}\n\nsql = {}\nparameters = {}".format(
                        str(e), variables["sql"], variables["parameters"]
                    )
                )
            else:
                raise
        if tracker is not None:
            db.table(table).transform(types=tracker.types)

        # Clean up open file-like objects
        if sniff_buffer:
            sniff_buffer.close()
        if decoded_buffer:
            decoded_buffer.close()


def _find_variables(tb, vars):
    to_find = list(vars)
    found = {}
    for var in to_find:
        if var in tb.tb_frame.f_locals:
            vars.remove(var)
            found[var] = tb.tb_frame.f_locals[var]
    if vars and tb.tb_next:
        found.update(_find_variables(tb.tb_next, vars))
    return found

CONTEXT_SETTINGS = dict(help_option_names=["-h", "--help"])


def _register_db_for_cleanup(db):
    """Register a database to be closed when the Click context is cleaned up."""
    ctx = click.get_current_context(silent=True)
    if ctx is None:
        return
    if "_databases_to_close" not in ctx.meta:
        ctx.meta["_databases_to_close"] = []
        ctx.call_on_close(lambda: _close_databases(ctx))
    ctx.meta["_databases_to_close"].append(db)


def _close_databases(ctx):
    """Close all databases registered for cleanup."""
    for db in ctx.meta.get("_databases_to_close", []):
        try:
            db.close()
        except Exception:
            pass

The input you provided uses a character encoding other than utf-8.

You can fix this by passing the --encoding= option with the encoding of the file.

If you do not know the encoding, running 'file filename.csv' may tell you.

It's often worth trying: --encoding=latin-1
""".strip()

def enable_wal(path, load_extension):
    """Enable WAL for database files

    Example:

    \b
        sqlite-utils enable-wal chickens.db
    """
    for path_ in path:
        db = sqlite_utils.Database(path_)
        _register_db_for_cleanup(db)
        _load_extensions(db, load_extension)
        db.enable_wal()


@cli.command(name="disable-wal")
@click.argument(
    "path",
    nargs=-1,
    type=click.Path(exists=True, file_okay=True, dir_okay=False, allow_dash=False),
    required=True,
)
@load_extension_option
def disable_wal(path, load_extension):
    """Disable WAL for database files

    Example:

    \b
        sqlite-utils disable-wal chickens.db
    """
    for path_ in path:
        db = sqlite_utils.Database(path_)
        _register_db_for_cleanup(db)
        _load_extensions(db, load_extension)
        db.disable_wal()


@cli.command(name="enable-counts")
@click.argument(
    "path",
    type=click.Path(exists=True, file_okay=True, dir_okay=False, allow_dash=False),
    required=True,
)
@click.argument("tables", nargs=-1)
@load_extension_option
def enable_counts(path, tables, load_extension):
    """Configure triggers to update a _counts table with row counts

    Example:

    \b
        sqlite-utils enable-counts chickens.db
    """
    db = sqlite_utils.Database(path)
    _register_db_for_cleanup(db)
    _load_extensions(db, load_extension)
    if not tables:
        db.enable_counts()
    else:
        # Check all tables exist
        bad_tables = [table for table in tables if not db[table].exists()]
        if bad_tables:
            raise click.ClickException("Invalid tables: {}".format(bad_tables))
        for table in tables:
            db.table(table).enable_counts()

    \b
        sqlite-utils reset-counts chickens.db
    """
    db = sqlite_utils.Database(path)

@cli.command(name="add-import-invariant")
@click.argument(
    "path",
    type=click.Path(file_okay=True, dir_okay=False, allow_dash=False),
    required=True,
)
@click.argument("table")
@click.argument("sql")
def add_import_invariant(path, table, sql):
    """Add an import invariant to a table

    The invariant is a SQL expression that must hold after a safe import,
    for example ``COUNT(*) > 0``.

    Example:

    \\b
        sqlite-utils add-import-invariant data.db dogs 'COUNT(*) > 0'
    """
    db = sqlite_utils.Database(path)
    _register_db_for_cleanup(db)
    invariant_id = db.add_import_invariant(table, sql)
    click.echo(invariant_id)


@cli.command(name="remove-import-invariant")
@click.argument(
    "path",
    type=click.Path(file_okay=True, dir_okay=False, allow_dash=False),
    required=True,
)
@click.argument("table")
@click.argument("invariant_id")
def remove_import_invariant(path, table, invariant_id):
    """Remove an import invariant from a table

    Example:

    \\b
        sqlite-utils remove-import-invariant data.db dogs 1234567890abcdef
    """
    db = sqlite_utils.Database(path)
    _register_db_for_cleanup(db)
    db.remove_import_invariant(table, invariant_id)


@cli.command(name="list-import-invariants")
@click.argument(
    "path",
    type=click.Path(file_okay=True, dir_okay=False, allow_dash=False),
    required=True,
)
@click.argument("table")
def list_import_invariants(path, table):
    """List import invariants for a table

    Example:

    \\b
        sqlite-utils list-import-invariants data.db dogs
    """
    db = sqlite_utils.Database(path)
    _register_db_for_cleanup(db)
    for invariant in db.list_import_invariants(table):
        click.echo("{}	{}".format(invariant["id"], invariant["expression"]))


@cli.command(name="validate-import-invariants")
@click.argument(
    "path",
    type=click.Path(file_okay=True, dir_okay=False, allow_dash=False),
    required=True,
)
@click.argument("table")
def validate_import_invariants(path, table):
    """Validate import invariants for a table

    This command always exits 0. The output indicates whether validation
    passed or failed, and lists any failing invariant IDs.

    Example:

    \\b
        sqlite-utils validate-import-invariants data.db dogs
    """
    db = sqlite_utils.Database(path)
    _register_db_for_cleanup(db)
    result = db.validate_import_invariants(table)
    if result["valid"]:
        click.echo("PASS: all import invariants valid for {}".format(table))
    else:
        click.echo("FAIL: {} import invariant(s) invalid for {}".format(len(result["failures"]), table))
        for failure in result["failures"]:
            click.echo("  {}".format(failure["id"]))

    db.rollback_to_checkpoint(a)

except CheckpointNotActiveError:

OBSERVATION:
returncode=0
        new checkpoints can be created until safe import mode is enabled again.
        """
        self._safe_import_enabled = False

    def create_import_checkpoint(self) -> str:
        """
        Create a new rollback checkpoint and return its opaque id.

        Raises :class:`SafeImportNotEnabledError` if safe import mode has not
        been enabled. Nested checkpoints are supported.
        """
        if not self._safe_import_enabled:
            raise SafeImportNotEnabledError(
                "Safe import mode is not enabled - call enable_safe_import() first"
            )
        checkpoint_id = uuid.uuid4().hex
        savepoint_name = "cp_{}".format(checkpoint_id)
        self.execute('SAVEPOINT "{}"'.format(savepoint_name))
        self._active_checkpoints[checkpoint_id] = savepoint_name
        self._checkpoint_order.append(checkpoint_id)
        return checkpoint_id

    def _checkpoint_not_active_or_missing(self, checkpoint_id: str) -> None:
        if checkpoint_id in self._finalized_checkpoints:
            raise CheckpointNotActiveError(
                "Import checkpoint {} has already been committed or rolled back".format(
                    checkpoint_id
                )
            )
        if checkpoint_id not in self._active_checkpoints:
            raise CheckpointNotFoundError(
                "Import checkpoint {} does not exist".format(checkpoint_id)
            )

    def rollback_to_checkpoint(self, checkpoint_id: str) -> None:
        """
        Roll back the database - including schema changes - to the state it was
        in when ``checkpoint_id`` was created.
        """
        self._checkpoint_not_active_or_missing(checkpoint_id)
        idx = self._checkpoint_order.index(checkpoint_id)
        savepoint_name = self._active_checkpoints[checkpoint_id]
        self.execute(
            'ROLLBACK TO SAVEPOINT "{}"'.format(savepoint_name)
        )
        self.execute('RELEASE SAVEPOINT "{}"'.format(savepoint_name))
        # Any checkpoints created after this one are destroyed too
        for later_id in self._checkpoint_order[idx:]:
            self._active_checkpoints.pop(later_id, None)
            if later_id not in self._finalized_checkpoints:
                self._finalized_checkpoints.add(later_id)
        self._checkpoint_order = self._checkpoint_order[:idx]

    def commit_checkpoint(self, checkpoint_id: str) -> None:
        """
        Commit a checkpoint, making all of its changes permanent and finalizing
        the checkpoint id.
        """
        self._checkpoint_not_active_or_missing(checkpoint_id)
        idx = self._checkpoint_order.index(checkpoint_id)
        savepoint_name = self._active_checkpoints[checkpoint_id]
        self.execute('RELEASE SAVEPOINT "{}"'.format(savepoint_name))
        # Nested checkpoints are released along with this one
        for later_id in self._checkpoint_order[idx:]:
            self._active_checkpoints.pop(later_id, None)
            if later_id not in self._finalized_checkpoints:
                self._finalized_checkpoints.add(later_id)
        self._checkpoint_order = self._checkpoint_order[:idx]

    def cleanup_checkpoint(self, checkpoint_id: str) -> None:
        """
        Remove all tracking for a checkpoint. If the checkpoint is still active
        it is released (committed) first so no dangling savepoint remains.
        """
        if checkpoint_id in self._active_checkpoints:
            savepoint_name = self._active_checkpoints[checkpoint_id]

END STEP

    def _ensure_invariants_table(self) -> None:
        with self._conn_context():
            self.execute(_IMPORT_INVARIANTS_CREATE_SQL.format(_IMPORT_INVARIANTS_TABLE))

    def add_import_invariant(self, table: str, sql: str) -> str:
        """
        Add a persistent import invariant for ``table`` and return an opaque
        invariant id.
        """
        self._ensure_invariants_table()
        invariant_id = uuid.uuid4().hex
        with self._conn_context():
            self.execute(
                'INSERT INTO {} ("table", "id", "expression") VALUES (?, ?, ?)'.format(
                    quote_identifier(_IMPORT_INVARIANTS_TABLE)
                ),
                [table, invariant_id, sql],
            )
=== run_safe_import ===
        Run ``fn`` inside a rollback checkpoint, validate import invariants,
        and commit only on success.

        Returns ``{"success": True}`` or, for non-strict mode,
        ``{"success": False, "checkpoint_id": ..., "failures": [...],
        "error_report": ...}``. In strict mode failures roll back and raise.
        """
        self.enable_safe_import()
        checkpoint_id = self.create_import_checkpoint()
        old_safe_active = self._safe_import_active
        self._safe_import_active = True
        try:
            try:
                fn()
            except Exception as e:
                self._safe_import_active = old_safe_active
                try:
                    self.rollback_to_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)
                    self.cleanup_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)
                except Exception:
                    pass
                if strict:
                    raise
                return {
                    "success": False,
                    "checkpoint_id": checkpoint_id,
                    "failures": [],
                    "error_report": str(e),
                }
            self._safe_import_active = old_safe_active
            tables_to_validate = tables if tables is not None else (
                [table] if table is not None else self._tables_with_invariants()
            )
            failures: List[Dict[str, str]] = []
            for t in tables_to_validate:
                failures.extend(self.validate_import_invariants(t)["failures"])
            if not failures:
                self.commit_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)
                self.cleanup_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)
                return {"success": True}
            self.rollback_to_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)
            self.cleanup_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)
            error_report = self._format_invariant_failure_report(failures)
            if strict:
                raise ImportInvariantError(error_report)
            return {
                "success": False,
                "checkpoint_id": checkpoint_id,
                "failures": failures,
                "error_report": error_report,
            }
        finally:
            self._safe_import_active = old_safe_active

    def execute_returning_dicts(
        self, sql: str, params: Optional[Union[Sequence, Dict[str, Any]]] = None
    ) -> List[dict]:
        return list(self.query(sql, params))

    def resolve_foreign_keys(
        self, name: str, foreign_keys: ForeignKeysType

END STEP

            error_report = self._format_invariant_failure_report(failures)
            if strict:
                raise ImportInvariantError(error_report)
            return {
                "success": False,
                "checkpoint_id": checkpoint_id,
                "failures": failures,
                "error_report": error_report,
            }
        finally:
            self._safe_import_active = old_safe_active
'''
new = '''        self.enable_safe_import()
        checkpoint_id = self.create_import_checkpoint()
        try:
            try:
                fn()
            except Exception as e:
                try:
                    self.rollback_to_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)
                    self.cleanup_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)
                except Exception:
                    pass
                if strict:
                    raise
                return {
                    "success": False,
                    "checkpoint_id": checkpoint_id,
                    "failures": [],
                    "error_report": str(e),
                }
            tables_to_validate = tables if tables is not None else (
                [table] if table is not None else self._tables_with_invariants()
            )
            failures: List[Dict[str, str]] = []
            for t in tables_to_validate:
                failures.extend(self.validate_import_invariants(t)["failures"])
            if not failures:
                self.commit_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)
                self.cleanup_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)
                return {"success": True}
            self.rollback_to_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)
            self.cleanup_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)
            error_report = self._format_invariant_failure_report(failures)
            if strict:
                raise ImportInvariantError(error_report)
            return {
                "success": False,
                "checkpoint_id": checkpoint_id,
                "failures": failures,
                "error_report": error_report,
            }
        finally:
            # Defensive: never leave safe-import mode active if something
            # unexpected happened and no checkpoints remain.
            self._clear_safe_import_active_if_no_checkpoints()
'''
assert old in src
src = src.replace(old, new, 1)

open(path, 'w').write(src)
print("done")

from sqlite_utils.db import Database, CheckpointNotActiveError, CheckpointNotFoundError

db.enable_safe_import()
a = db.create_import_checkpoint()
db['t'].create({'id': int}, pk='id')

    print('b correctly not active')

x = db2.create_import_checkpoint()

db2['t2'].insert({'id': 1})

print('after rollback x: t2 exists?', db2['t2'].exists())

    db2.commit_checkpoint(y)
except CheckpointNotActiveError:

new = '''By default all of the SQL queries will be executed in a single transaction. To commit every 20 records, use ``--batch-size 20``.

.. _cli_safe_imports:

Safe imports
============

Bulk imports can partially fail, leaving a database inconsistent. Safe import mode
runs an import inside a rollback checkpoint, validates :ref:`import invariants
<cli_import_invariants>` after the writes and commits only if they all pass. On any
failure the database - including schema changes such as tables, columns, indexes and
triggers - is rolled back to its exact pre-operation state.

The ``insert``, ``upsert`` and ``bulk`` commands accept ``--safe-mode``. With
``--safe-mode`` the format flags (``--json``, ``--nl``, ``--csv``, ``--tsv``,
``--lines`` and ``--text``) are optional - the format is inferred from the input
filename where possible, defaulting to JSON.

For example, this import will only be committed if the ``dogs`` table ends up
with more than zero rows:

.. code-block:: bash

    sqlite-utils add-import-invariant data.db dogs 'COUNT(*) > 0'
    sqlite-utils insert data.db dogs dogs.csv --csv --safe-mode

The ``bulk`` command also supports ``--safe-mode`` for UPDATE and other SQL
statements:

.. code-block:: bash

    sqlite-utils bulk data.db \\
      'update dogs set name = :name where id = :id' \\
      dogs.csv --csv --safe-mode

Safe import mode can be enabled and disabled for a database:

.. code-block:: bash

    sqlite-utils enable-safe-import data.db
    sqlite-utils disable-safe-import data.db

.. _cli_import_invariants:

Import invariants
-----------------

Import invariants are persistent SQL expressions attached to a table. A safe
import validates them after writing and rolls back if any of them are false.

Add an invariant with ``add-import-invariant``:

.. code-block:: bash

    sqlite-utils add-import-invariant data.db dogs 'COUNT(*) > 0'

Remove an invariant with ``remove-import-invariant``:

.. code-block:: bash

    sqlite-utils remove-import-invariant data.db dogs INVARIANT_ID

List invariants with ``list-import-invariants`` - this prints the invariant id
followed by a tab and the SQL expression:

.. code-block:: bash

    sqlite-utils list-import-invariants data.db dogs

Validate invariants with ``validate-import-invariants``. This command always
exits 0; the output indicates whether validation passed or failed and lists any
failing invariant ids:

.. code-block:: bash

    sqlite-utils validate-import-invariants data.db dogs

.. _cli_insert_files:
'''
assert old in src
src = src.replace(old, new, 1)
open(path, 'w').write(src)
print("done")

====

::

    Usage: sqlite-utils rows [OPTIONS] PATH DBTABLE

      Output all rows in the specified table

      Example:

          sqlite-utils rows trees.db Trees

    Options:
      -c, --column TEXT           Columns to return
      --where TEXT                Optional where clause
      -o, --order TEXT            Order by ('column' or 'column desc')
      -p, --param <TEXT TEXT>...  Named :parameters for where clause
      --limit INTEGER             Number of rows to return - defaults to everything
      --offset INTEGER            SQL offset to use
      --nl                        Output newline-delimited JSON
      --arrays                    Output rows as arrays instead of objects
      --csv                       Output CSV
      --tsv                       Output TSV
      --no-headers                Omit CSV headers
      -t, --table                 Output as a formatted table
      --fmt TEXT                  Table format - one of asciidoc, double_grid,
                                  double_outline, fancy_grid, fancy_outline, github,
                                  grid, heavy_grid, heavy_outline, html, jira,
                                  latex, latex_booktabs, latex_longtable, latex_raw,
                                  mediawiki, mixed_grid, mixed_outline, moinmoin,
                                  orgtbl, outline, pipe, plain, presto, pretty,
                                  psql, rounded_grid, rounded_outline, rst, simple,
                                  simple_grid, simple_outline, textile, tsv,

      --json-cols                 Detect JSON cols and output them as JSON, not

.. _cli_ref_remove_import_invariant:

remove-import-invariant

See :ref:`cli_import_invariants`.

::

    Usage: sqlite-utils remove-import-invariant [OPTIONS] PATH TABLE
                                                INVARIANT_ID

      Remove an import invariant from a table

      Example:

          sqlite-utils remove-import-invariant data.db dogs 1234567890abcdef

    Options:
      -h, --help  Show this message and exit.

OBSERVATION:
returncode=0
    def quote(self, value: str) -> str:
        """
        Apply SQLite string quoting to a value, including wrapping it in single quotes.

        :param value: String to quote
        """
        # Normally we would use .execute(sql, [params]) for escaping, but
        # occasionally that isn't available - most notable when we need
        # to include a "... DEFAULT 'value'" in a column definition.
        return self.execute(
            # Use SQLite itself to correctly escape this string:
            "SELECT quote(:value)",
            {"value": value},
        ).fetchone()[0]

    def quote_fts(self, query: str) -> str:
        """
        Escape special characters in a SQLite full-text search query.

        This works by surrounding each token within the query with double
        quotes, in order to avoid words like ``NOT`` and ``OR`` having
        special meaning as defined by the FTS query syntax here:

        https://www.sqlite.org/fts5.html#full_text_query_syntax

        If the query has unbalanced ``"`` characters, adds one at end.

        :param query: String to escape
        """
        if query.count('"') % 2:
            query += '"'
        bits = _quote_fts_re.split(query)
        bits = [b for b in bits if b and b != '""']
        return " ".join(
            '"{}"'.format(bit) if not bit.startswith('"') else bit for bit in bits
        )

    def quote_default_value(self, value: str) -> str:
        if any(
            [
                str(value).startswith("'") and str(value).endswith("'"),
                str(value).startswith('"') and str(value).endswith('"'),
            ]

713:    def table_names(self, fts4: bool = False, fts5: bool = False) -> List[str]:
    def table_names(self, fts4: bool = False, fts5: bool = False) -> List[str]:
        """
        List of string table names in this database.

        :param fts4: Only return tables that are part of FTS4 indexes
        :param fts5: Only return tables that are part of FTS5 indexes
        """
        where = ["type = 'table'"]
        if fts4:
            where.append("sql like '%USING FTS4%'")
        if fts5:
            where.append("sql like '%USING FTS5%'")
        sql = "select name from sqlite_master where {}".format(" AND ".join(where))
        return [r[0] for r in self.execute(sql).fetchall()]

    def view_names(self) -> List[str]:
        "List of string view names in this database."

+    @contextlib.contextmanager
+    def _conn_context(self) -> Generator[None, None, None]:
+        """
+        Context manager used internally for transactions.
+
+        During a safe import the inner commit normally triggered by
+        ``with self.conn`` is suppressed, so that all writes - including
+        schema changes - stay inside the active rollback checkpoint.
+        """
+        if self._safe_import_active:
+            yield
+        else:
+            with self.conn:
+                yield

+    def enable_safe_import(self) -> None:
+        """
+        Enable safe import mode for this database. While enabled,
+        :meth:`create_import_checkpoint` can be used to create rollback
+        checkpoints.
+        """
+        self._safe_import_enabled = True
+
+    def disable_safe_import(self) -> None:
+        """
+        Disable safe import mode. Existing checkpoints are unaffected, but no
+        new checkpoints can be created until safe import mode is enabled again.
+        """
+        self._safe_import_enabled = False
+
+    def create_import_checkpoint(self) -> str:
+        """
+        Create a new rollback checkpoint and return its opaque id.
+
+        Raises :class:`SafeImportNot
[OUTPUT_TAIL]
has not
+        been enabled. Nested checkpoints are supported.
+        """
+        if not self._safe_import_enabled:
+            raise SafeImportNotEnabledError(
+                "Safe import mode is not enabled - call enable_safe_import() first"
+            )
+        checkpoint_id = uuid.uuid4().hex
+        savepoint_name = "cp_{}".format(checkpoint_id)
+        self.execute('SAVEPOINT "{}"'.format(savepoint_name))
+        self._active_checkpoints[checkpoint_id] = savepoint_name
+        self._checkpoint_order.append(checkpoint_id)
+        # While any checkpoint is active, inner ``with self.conn`` blocks must
+        # not commit, otherwise they would destroy the savepoint.
+        self._safe_import_active = True
+        return checkpoint_id
+
+    def _checkpoint_not_active_or_missing(self, checkpoint_id: str) -> None:
+        if checkpoint_id in self._finalized_checkpoints:
+            raise CheckpointNotActiveError(
+                "Import checkpoint {} has already been committed or rolled back".format(
+                    checkpoint_id
+                )
+            )
+        if checkpoint_id not in self._active_checkpoints:
+            raise CheckpointNotFoundError(
+                "Import checkpoint {} does not exist".format(checkpoint_id)
+            )
+
+    def rollback_to_checkpoint(self, checkpoint_id: str) -> None:
+        """
+        Roll back the database - including schema changes - to the state it was
+        in when ``checkpoint_id`` was created.
+        """
+        self._checkpoint_not_active_or_missing(checkpoint_id)
+        idx = self._checkpoint_order.index(checkpoint_id)
+        savepoint_name = self._active_checkpoints[checkpoint_id]
+        self.execute(
+            'ROLLBACK TO SAVEPOINT "{}"'.format(savepoint_name)
+        )
+        self.execute('RELEASE SAVEPOINT "{}"'.format(savepoint_name))
+        # Any checkpoints created after this one are destroyed too
+        for later_id in self._checkpoint_order[idx:]:
+            self._active_checkpoints.pop(later_id, None)
+            if later_id not in self._finalized_checkpoints:
+                self._finalized_checkpoints.add(later_id)
+        self._checkpoint_order = self._checkpoint_order[:idx]
+        self._clear_safe_import_active_if_no_checkpoints()
+
+    def _clear_safe_import_active_if_no_checkpoints(self) -> None:
+        if not self._active_checkpoints:
+            self._safe_import_active = False
+
+    def commit_checkpoint(self, checkpoint_id: str) -> None:
+        """
+        Commit a checkpoint, making all of its changes permanent and finalizing
+        the checkpoint id.
+        """
+        self._checkpoint_not_active_or_missing(checkpoint_id)
+        idx = self._checkpoint_order.index(checkpoint_id)
+        savepoint_name = self._active_checkpoints[checkpoint_id]
+        self.execute('RELEASE SAVEPOINT "{}"'.format(savepoint_name))
+        # Nested checkpoints are released along with this one
+        for later_id in self._checkpoint_order[idx:]:
+            self._active_checkpoints.pop(later_id, None)
+            if later_id not in self._finalized_checkpoints:
+                self._finalized_checkpoints.add(later_id)
+        self._checkpoint_order = self._checkpoint_order[:idx]
+        self._clear_safe_import_active_if_no_checkpoints()
+
+    def cleanup_checkpoint(self, checkpoint_id: str) -> None:
+        """
+        Remove all tracking for a checkpoint. If the checkpoint is still active
+        it is released (committed) first so no dangling savepoint remains.
+        """
+        if checkpoint_id in self._active_checkpoints:
+            savepoint_name = self._active_checkpoints[checkpoint_id]
+            try:
+                self.execute('RELEASE SAVEPOINT "{}"'.format(savepoint_name))
+            except OperationalError:
+                pass
+            idx = self._checkpoint_order.index(checkpoint_id)
+            self._checkpoint_order = [
+                cid for cid in self._checkpoint_order if cid != checkpoint_id
+            ]
+            self._active_checkpoints.pop(checkpoint_id, None)
+            self._clear_safe_import_active_if_no_checkpoints()
+            return
+        if checkpoint_id in self._finalized_checkpoints:
+            self._finalized_checkpoints.remove(checkpoint_id)
+            return
+        raise CheckpointNotFoundError(
+            "Import checkpoint {} does not exist".format(checkpoint_id)
+        )
+
+    def _ensure_invariants_table(self) -> None:
+        with self._conn_context():
+            self.execute(_IMPORT_INVARIANTS_CREATE_SQL.format(_IMPORT_INVARIANTS_TABLE))
+
+    def add_import_invariant(self, table: str, sql: str) -> str:
+        """
+        Add a persistent import invariant for ``table`` and return an opaque
+        invariant id.
+        """
+        self._ensure_invariants_table()
+        invariant_id = uuid.uuid4().hex
+        with self._conn_context():
+            self.execute(

[ARCHIVE ELIDED 34 CHARS]

cd /app && cat > /tmp/test_safe_import.py <<'EOF'
import io
import pytest
from sqlite_utils import Database
from sqlite_utils.db import (
    SafeImportNotEnabledError,
    CheckpointNotActiveError,
    CheckpointNotFoundError,
    ImportInvariantError,
)

def test_checkpoints():
    db = Database(memory=True)
    with pytest.raises(SafeImportNotEnabledError):
        db.create_import_checkpoint()
    db.enable_safe_import()
    cid = db.create_import_checkpoint()
    assert isinstance(cid, str) and cid
    db.rollback_to_checkpoint(cid)
    with pytest.raises(CheckpointNotActiveError):
        db.rollback_to_checkpoint(cid)
    db.cleanup_checkpoint(cid)
    with pytest.raises(CheckpointNotFoundError):
        db.commit_checkpoint(cid)
    with pytest.raises(CheckpointNotFoundError):
        db.rollback_to_checkpoint(cid)
    with pytest.raises(CheckpointNotFoundError):
        db.cleanup_checkpoint("nope")

def test_nested_checkpoints():
    db = Database(memory=True)
    db.enable_safe_import()
    a = db.create_import_checkpoint()
    db['t'].create({'id': int}, pk='id')
    b = db.create_import_checkpoint()
    db['t'].insert({'id': 1})
    db.rollback_to_checkpoint(b)
    assert db['t'].count == 0
    db.commit_checkpoint(a)
    assert db['t'].exists()
    with pytest.raises(CheckpointNotActiveError):
        db.rollback_to_checkpoint(b)

def test_schema_rollback():
    db = Database(memory=True)
    db['t'].create({'id': int}, pk='id')
    db.add_import_invariant('t', 'COUNT(*) <= 1')
    r = db['t'].safe_bulk_insert([{'id': 2}, {'id': 3}])
    assert r['success'] is False
    assert 'checkpoint_id' in r
    assert isinstance(r['failures'], list)
    assert isinstance(r['error_report'], str)
    assert db['t'].count == 0

def test_strict_invariant_message():
    db = Database(memory=True)
    db['t'].create({'id': int}, pk='id')
    db.add_import_invariant('t', 'COUNT(*) <= 1')
    with pytest.raises(ImportInvariantError) as exc:
        db['t'].safe_bulk_insert([{'id': 2}], strict=True)
    msg = str(exc.value)
    assert 'valid' in msg or 'validation' in msg or 'invariant' in msg

def test_invariant_eval():
    db = Database(memory=True)
    db['t'].create({'id': int, 'price': int})
    db['t'].insert_all([{'id': 1, 'price': 5}, {'id': 2, 'price': 10}])
    assert db.validate_import_invariants('t') == {'valid': True, 'failures': []}
    i1 = db.add_import_invariant('t', 'COUNT(*) = 2')
    i2 = db.add_import_invariant('t', 'price > 0')
    i3 = db.add_import_invariant('t', 'SELECT COUNT(*) FROM t WHERE id > 0')
    i4 = db.add_import_invariant('t', 'MIN(price) >= 5')
    assert db.validate_import_invariants('t')['valid'] is True
    db.remove_import_invariant('t', i1)
    assert len(db.list_import_invariants('t')) == 3
    # failing invariant
    i5 = db.add_import_invariant('t', 'price > 5')
    result = db.validate_import_invariants('t')
    assert result['valid'] is False
    assert any(f['id'] == i5 for f in result['failures'])

def test_import_csv_json():
    db = Database(memory=True)
    db.import_csv('people', io.StringIO('id,name\n1,a\n2,b\n'))
    assert db['people'].count == 2
    db.import_json('people', [{'id': 3, 'name': 'c'}])
    assert db['people'].count == 3
    db.add_import_invariant('people', 'COUNT(*) <= 2')
    r = db.import_json('people', [{'id': 4, 'name': 'd'}], safe_mode=True)
    assert r['success'] is False
    assert db['people'].count == 3

def test_safe_bulk_upsert():
    db = Database(memory=True)
    db['t'].create({'id': int, 'name': str}, pk='id')
    db['t'].insert({'id': 1, 'name': 'a'})
    db.add_import_invariant('t', 'COUNT(*) <= 1')
    r = db['t'].safe_bulk_upsert([{'id': 2, 'name': 'b'}], pk='id')
    assert r['success'] is False
    assert db['t'].count == 1
    r2 = db['t'].safe_bulk_upsert([{'id': 1, 'name': 'x'}], pk='id')
    assert r2['success'] is True
    assert db['t'].get(1)['name'] == 'x'

returncode=0
.. _python_api_bulk_inserts:

Bulk inserts
============

If you have more than one record to insert, the ``insert_all()`` method is a much more efficient way of inserting them. Just like ``insert()`` it will automatically detect the columns that should be created, but it will inspect the first batch of 100 items to help decide what those column types should be.

Use it like this:

.. code-block:: python

    db.table("dogs").insert_all([{
        "id": 1,
        "name": "Cleo",
        "twitter": "cleopaws",
        "age": 3,
        "is_good_dog": True,
    }, {
        "id": 2,
        "name": "Marnie",
        "twitter": "MarnieTheDog",
        "age": 16,
        "is_good_dog": True,
    }], pk="id", column_order=("id", "twitter", "name"))

The column types used in the ``CREATE TABLE`` statement are automatically derived from the types of data in that first batch of rows. Any additional columns in subsequent batches will cause a ``sqlite3.OperationalError`` exception to be raised unless the ``alter=True`` argument is supplied, in which case the new columns will be created.

The function can accept an iterator or generator of rows and will commit them according to the batch size. The default batch size is 100, but you can specify a different size using the ``batch_size`` parameter:

.. code-block:: python

    db.table("big_table").insert_all(({
        "id": 1,
        "name": "Name {}".format(i),
    } for i in range(10000)), batch_size=1000)

You can skip inserting any records that have a primary key that already exists using ``ignore=True``. This works with both ``.insert({...}, ignore=True)`` and ``.insert_all([...], ignore=True)``.

You can delete all the existing rows in the table before inserting the new records using ``truncate=True``. This is useful if you want to replace the data in the table.

Pass ``analyze=True`` to run ``ANALYZE`` against the table after inserting the new records.

.. _python_api_insert_lists:


END STEP

.. _python_api_safe_imports:

Safe imports
============

Bulk imports can partially fail, leaving a database inconsistent. Safe import
mode runs an import inside a rollback checkpoint, validates import invariants
after the writes and commits only if they all pass. On any failure the database
- including schema changes such as tables, columns, indexes and triggers - is
rolled back to its exact pre-operation state.

Import invariants are persistent SQL expressions attached to a table:

.. code-block:: python

    db = Database("dogs.db")
    invariant_id = db.add_import_invariant("dogs", "COUNT(*) > 0")
    db.list_import_invariants("dogs")
    # [{"id": "...", "expression": "COUNT(*) > 0"}]
    db.validate_import_invariants("dogs")
    # {"valid": True, "failures": []}
    db.remove_import_invariant("dogs", invariant_id)

Safe bulk operations are available on tables:

.. code-block:: python

    result = db.table("dogs").safe_bulk_insert(records, pk="id")
    # {"success": True}
    # or {"success": False, "checkpoint_id": "...", "failures": [...],
    #     "error_report": "..."}

    result = db.table("dogs").safe_bulk_upsert(records, pk="id")

With ``strict=True`` failures roll back and raise an exception. CSV and JSON
imports can also run in safe mode:

.. code-block:: python

    db.import_csv("dogs", "dogs.csv", safe_mode=True)
    db.import_json("dogs", data, safe_mode=True)

Rollback checkpoints can be used directly:

.. code-block:: python

    db.enable_safe_import()
    checkpoint_id = db.create_import_checkpoint()
    # ... make changes ...
    db.rollback_to_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)  # or db.commit_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)
    db.cleanup_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)
    db.disable_safe_import()

cd /app && cat > tests/test_safe_import.py <<'EOF'
import io

import pytest

from sqlite_utils import Database
from sqlite_utils.db import (
    CheckpointNotActiveError,
    CheckpointNotFoundError,
    ImportInvariantError,
    SafeImportNotEnabledError,
)


def test_checkpoint_requires_enabled_safe_import():
    db = Database(memory=True)
    with pytest.raises(SafeImportNotEnabledError):
        db.create_import_checkpoint()


def test_checkpoint_commit_rollback_rules():
    db = Database(memory=True)
    db.enable_safe_import()
    checkpoint_id = db.create_import_checkpoint()
    assert isinstance(checkpoint_id, str) and checkpoint_id
    db.commit_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)
    with pytest.raises(CheckpointNotActiveError):
        db.commit_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)
    with pytest.raises(CheckpointNotActiveError):
        db.rollback_to_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)
    db.cleanup_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)
    with pytest.raises(CheckpointNotFoundError):
        db.commit_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)
    with pytest.raises(CheckpointNotFoundError):
        db.rollback_to_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)


def test_checkpoint_rollback_schema():
    db = Database(memory=True)
    db.enable_safe_import()
    checkpoint_id = db.create_import_checkpoint()
    db["t"].create({"id": int}, pk="id")
    db.execute("CREATE INDEX idx_t ON t(id)")
    db.execute("CREATE TRIGGER trg AFTER INSERT ON t BEGIN SELECT 1; END")
    db["t"].insert({"id": 1})
    db.rollback_to_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)
    db.cleanup_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)
    assert "t" not in db.table_names()
    assert db.triggers_dict == {}


def test_nested_checkpoints():
    db = Database(memory=True)
    db.enable_safe_import()
    outer = db.create_import_checkpoint()
    db["t"].create({"id": int}, pk="id")
    inner = db.create_import_checkpoint()
    db["t"].insert({"id": 1})
    db.rollback_to_checkpoint(inner)
    assert db["t"].count == 0
    db.commit_checkpoint(outer)
    assert db["t"].exists()
    with pytest.raises(CheckpointNotActiveError):
        db.commit_checkpoint(inner)


def test_import_invariants_crud_and_validation():
    db = Database(memory=True)
    db["t"].create({"id": int, "price": int})
    db["t"].insert_all([{"id": 1, "price": 5}, {"id": 2, "price": 10}])
    assert db.list_import_invariants("t") == []
    invariant_id = db.add_import_invariant("t", "COUNT(*) = 2")
    assert db.list_import_invariants("t") == [
        {"id": invariant_id, "expression": "COUNT(*) = 2"}
    ]
    assert db.validate_import_invariants("t") == {"valid": True, "failures": []}
    failing = db.add_import_invariant("t", "price > 5")
    result = db.validate_import_invariants("t")
    assert result["valid"] is False
    assert any(f["id"] == failing for f in result["failures"])
    assert all(
        set(f) == {"id", "expression", "error"} for f in result["failures"]
    )
    db.remove_import_invariant("t", failing)
    assert db.validate_import_invariants("t")["valid"] is True


def test_safe_bulk_insert_rolls_back_on_invariant_failure():
    db = Database(memory=True)
    db["t"].create({"id": int}, pk="id")
    db["t"].insert({"id": 1})
    db.add_import_invariant("t", "COUNT(*) <= 1")
    result = db["t"].safe_bulk_insert([{"id": 2}, {"id": 3}])
    assert result["success"] is False
    assert "checkpoint_id" in result
    assert "failures" in result
    assert "error_report" in result
    assert db["t"].count == 1


def test_safe_bulk_insert_strict_raises_invariant_error():
    db = Database(memory=True)
    db["t"].create({"id": int}, pk="id")
    db.add_import_invariant("t", "COUNT(*) <= 0")
    with pytest.raises(ImportInvariantError) as exc:
        db["t"].safe_bulk_insert([{"id": 1}], strict=True)
    message = str(exc.value).lower()
    assert "valid" in message or "validation" in message or "invariant" in message
    assert db["t"].count == 0


def test_safe_bulk_upsert():
    db = Database(memory=True)
    db["t"].create({"id": int, "name": str}, pk="id")
    db["t"].insert({"id": 1, "name": "before"})
    db.add_import_invariant("t", "COUNT(*) <= 1")
    result = db["t"].safe_bulk_upsert([{"id": 2, "name": "new"}], pk="id")
    assert result["success"] is False
    assert db["t"].count == 1
    result = db["t"].safe_bulk_upsert([{"id": 1, "name": "after"}], pk="id")
    assert result["success"] is True
    assert db["t"].get(1)["name"] == "after"


def test_import_csv_and_json():
    db = Database(memory=True)
    assert db.import_csv("people", io.StringIO("id,name\n1,Alice\n2,Bob\n")) == {
        "success": True
    }
    assert db["people"].count == 2
    assert db.import_json("people", [{"id": 3, "name": "Carol"}]) == {"success": True}
    assert db["people"].count == 3
    db.add_import_invariant("people", "COUNT(*) <= 2")
    result = db.import_json("people", [{"id": 4, "name": "Dave"}], safe_mode=True)
    assert result["success"] is False
    assert db["people"].count == 3

COMMAND:
cd /app && sed -n '909,1010p' sqlite_utils/db.py && echo "=== run_safe_import ===" && sed -n '1142,1210p' sqlite_utils/db.py
OBSERVATION:
returncode=0
    def create_import_checkpoint(self) -> str:
        """
        Create a new rollback checkpoint and return its opaque id.

        Raises :class:`SafeImportNotEnabledError` if safe import mode has not
        been enabled. Nested checkpoints are supported.
        """
        if not self._safe_import_enabled:
            raise SafeImportNotEnabledError(
                "Safe import mode is not enabled - call enable_safe_import() first"
            )
        checkpoint_id = uuid.uuid4().hex
        savepoint_name = "cp_{}".format(checkpoint_id)
        self.execute('SAVEPOINT "{}"'.format(savepoint_name))
        self._active_checkpoints[checkpoint_id] = savepoint_name
        self._checkpoint_order.append(checkpoint_id)
        # While any checkpoint is active, inner ``with self.conn`` blocks must
        # not commit, otherwise they would destroy the savepoint.
        self._safe_import_active = True
        return checkpoint_id

    def _checkpoint_not_active_or_missing(self, checkpoint_id: str) -> None:
        if checkpoint_id in self._finalized_checkpoints:
            raise CheckpointNotActiveError(
                "Import checkpoint {} has already been committed or rolled back".format(
                    checkpoint_id
                )
            )
        if checkpoint_id not in self._active_checkpoints:
            raise CheckpointNotFoundError(
                "Import checkpoint {} does not exist".format(checkpoint_id)
            )

    def rollback_to_checkpoint(self, checkpoint_id: str) -> None:
        """
        Roll back the database - including schema changes - to the state it was
        in when ``checkpoint_id`` was created.
        """
        self._checkpoint_not_active_or_missing(checkpoint_id)
        idx = self._checkpoint_order.index(checkpoint_id)
        savepoint_name = self._active_checkpoints[checkpoint_id]
        self.execute(
            'ROLLBACK TO SAVEPOINT "{}"'.format(savepoint_name)
        )
        self.execute('RELEASE SAVEPOINT "{}"'.format(savepoint_name))
        # Any checkpoints created after this one are destroyed too
        for later_id in self._checkpoint_order[idx:]:
            self._active_checkpoints.pop(later_id, None)
            if later_id not in self._finalized_checkpoints:
                self._finalized_checkpoints.add(later_id)
        self._checkpoint_order = self._checkpoint_order[:idx]
        self._clear_safe_import_active_if_no_checkpoints()

    def _clear_safe_import_active_if_no_checkpoints(self) -> None:
        if not self._active_checkpoints:
            self._safe_import_active = False

    def commit_checkpoint(self, checkpoint_id: str) -> None:
        """
        Commit a checkpoint, making all of its changes permanent and finalizing
        the checkpoint id.
        """
        self._checkpoint_not_active_or_missing(checkpoint_id)
        idx = self._checkpoint_order.index(checkpoint_id)
        savepoint_name = self._active_checkpoints[checkpoint_id]
        self.execute('RELEASE SAVEPOINT "{}"'.format(savepoint_name))
        # Nested checkpoints are released along with this one
        for later_id in self._checkpoint_order[idx:]:
            self._active_checkpoints.pop(later_id, None)
            if later_id not in self._finalized_checkpoints:
                self._finalized_checkpoints.add(later_id)
        self._checkpoint_order = self._checkpoint_order[:idx]
        self._clear_safe_import_active_if_no_checkpoints()

    def cleanup_checkpoint(self, checkpoint_id: str) -> None:
        """
        Remove all tracking for a checkpoint. If the checkpoint is still active
        it is released (committed) first so no dangling savepoint remains.
        """
        if checkpoint_id in self._active_checkpoints:
            savepoint_name = self._active_checkpoints[checkpoint_id]
            try:
                self.execute('RELEASE SAVEPOINT "{}"'.format(savepoint_name))
            except OperationalError:
                pass
            idx = self._checkpoint_order.index(checkpoint_id)
            # Nested checkpoints created after this one are released too
            for later_id in self._checkpoint_order[idx:]:
                self._active_checkpoints.pop(later_id, None)
                if later_id not in self._finalized_checkpoints:
                    self._finalized_checkpoints.add(later_id)
            self._checkpoint_order = self._checkpoint_order[:idx]
            self._clear_safe_import_active_if_no_checkpoints()
            return
        if checkpoint_id in self._finalized_checkpoints:
            self._finalized_checkpoints.remove(checkpoint_id)
            return
        raise CheckpointNotFoundError(
            "Import checkpoint {} does not exist".format(checkpoint_id)
        )

    def _ensure_invariants_table(self) -> None:
=== run_safe_import ===
        return "\n".join(lines)

    def run_safe_import(
        self,
        table: Optional[str],
        strict: bool,
        fn: Callable[[], None],
        tables: Optional[List[str]] = None,
    ) -> Dict[str, Any]:
        """
        Run ``fn`` inside a rollback checkpoint, validate import invariants,
        and commit only on success.

        Returns ``{"success": True}`` or, for non-strict mode,
        ``{"success": False, "checkpoint_id": ..., "failures": [...],
        "error_report": ...}``. In strict mode failures roll back and raise.
        """
        self.enable_safe_import()
        checkpoint_id = self.create_import_checkpoint()
        try:
            try:
                fn()
            except Exception as e:
                try:
                    self.rollback_to_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)
                    self.cleanup_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)
                except Exception:
                    pass
                if strict:
                    raise
                return {
                    "success": False,
                    "checkpoint_id": checkpoint_id,
                    "failures": [],
                    "error_report": str(e),
                }
            tables_to_validate = tables if tables is not None else (
                [table] if table is not None else self._tables_with_invariants()
            )
            failures: List[Dict[str, str]] = []
            for t in tables_to_validate:
                failures.extend(self.validate_import_invariants(t)["failures"])
            if not failures:
                self.commit_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)
                self.cleanup_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)
                return {"success": True}
            self.rollback_to_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)
            self.cleanup_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)
            error_report = self._format_invariant_failure_report(failures)
            if strict:
                raise ImportInvariantError(error_report)
            return {
                "success": False,
                "checkpoint_id": checkpoint_id,
                "failures": failures,
                "error_report": error_report,
            }
        finally:
            # Defensive: never leave safe-import mode active if something
            # unexpected happened and no checkpoints remain.
            self._clear_safe_import_active_if_no_checkpoints()

    def execute_returning_dicts(
        self, sql: str, params: Optional[Union[Sequence, Dict[str, Any]]] = None
    ) -> List[dict]:
        return list(self.query(sql, params))

    def resolve_foreign_keys(
        self, name: str, foreign_keys: ForeignKeysType

END STEP

returncode=0
This command takes the same options as the ``sqlite-utils insert`` command - so it defaults to expecting JSON but can accept other formats using ``--csv`` or ``--tsv`` or ``--nl`` or other options described above.

By default all of the SQL queries will be executed in a single transaction. To commit every 20 records, use ``--batch-size 20``.

.. _cli_safe_imports:

Safe imports
============

Bulk imports can partially fail, leaving a database inconsistent. Safe import mode
runs an import inside a rollback checkpoint, validates :ref:`import invariants
<cli_import_invariants>` after the writes and commits only if they all pass. On any
failure the database - including schema changes such as tables, columns, indexes and
triggers - is rolled back to its exact pre-operation state.

The ``insert``, ``upsert`` and ``bulk`` commands accept ``--safe-mode``. With
``--safe-mode`` the format flags (``--json``, ``--nl``, ``--csv``, ``--tsv``,
``--lines`` and ``--text``) are optional - the format is inferred from the input
filename where possible, defaulting to JSON.

For example, this import will only be committed if the ``dogs`` table ends up
with more than zero rows:

.. code-block:: bash

    sqlite-utils add-import-invariant data.db dogs 'COUNT(*) > 0'
    sqlite-utils insert data.db dogs dogs.csv --csv --safe-mode

The ``bulk`` command also supports ``--safe-mode`` for UPDATE and other SQL
statements:

.. code-block:: bash

    sqlite-utils bulk data.db \
      'update dogs set name = :name where id = :id' \
      dogs.csv --csv --safe-mode

Safe import mode can be enabled and disabled for a database:

.. code-block:: bash

    sqlite-utils enable-safe-import data.db
    sqlite-utils disable-safe-import data.db

.. _cli_import_invariants:

Import invariants
-----------------

Import invariants are persistent SQL expressions attached to a table. A safe
import validates them after writing and rolls back if any of them are false.

Add an invariant with ``add-import-invariant``:

.. code-block:: bash

    sqlite-utils add-import-invariant data.db dogs 'COUNT(*) > 0'

Remove an invariant with ``remove-import-invariant``:

.. code-block:: bash

    sqlite-utils remove-import-invariant data.db dogs INVARIANT_ID

List invariants with ``list-import-invariants`` - this prints the invariant id
followed by a tab and the SQL expression:

.. code-block:: bash

    sqlite-utils list-import-invariants data.db dogs

Validate invariants with ``validate-import-invariants``. This command always
exits 0; the output indicates whether validation passed or failed and lists any
failing invariant ids:

.. code-block:: bash

    sqlite-utils validate-import-invariants data.db dogs

.. _cli_insert_files:

Inserting data from files
=========================

The ``insert-files`` command can be used to insert the content of files, along with their metadata, into a SQLite table.

Here's an example that inserts all of the GIF files in the current directory into a ``gifs.db`` database, placing the file contents in an ``images`` table:

.. code-block:: bash

    sqlite-utils insert-files gifs.db images *.gif

END STEP
